Table 1:
Parameters of the amplifier (A) and cavity (C) modes extracted through catch-and-release measurements for each qubit state, as well as their qubit state-averaged values. Uncertainties quoted are
1\sigma
confidence intervals.

The vanishing dispersive shift of mode A is apparent in the near indistinguishability of the signal’s phase accumulation with respect to the qubit’s state in the AB beam-splitter measurement. By contrast, the BC beam-splitter data show a phase accumulation that is highly distinguishable with respect to the qubit state, due to the considerable dispersive shift of mode C. The amplitude decay and thus linewidth for both modes only slightly depends on qubit state, with mode C experiencing a larger variation due to its larger dispersive shift paired with a general frequency-dependence of the real part of the environmental impedance seen by the cavity.

As a brief aside, it is worth noting that the BC catch-and-release protocol can serve as a qubit measurement
Peronnin et al. 2020
. Assuming
\gamma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}}=0
and the ability to instantaneously inject signal into mode C, maximal SNR would be achieved for a delay time of
t_{\mathrm{ev,opt}}=\pi/2\chi_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}}\approx 90
ns. Experimentally, due to the finite
\gamma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}}
and effective C mode drive duration, we observe optimal SNR at a delay time of
t_{\mathrm{ev,opt}}\approx 40
ns.

Figure 12:
A and C mode characterization
. Simplified experimental sequence of and data for the catch-and-release protocols with
(a)
mode A and
(b)
mode C. The protocol includes qubit state preparation, a simultaneous B mode drive and beam-splitter between modes B and
\mathrm{K}=\mathrm{A},\mathrm{C}
, a time delay
t_{\mathrm{ev}}
of variable duration, and a BK beam splitter to release the measurement signal. Demodulating and shot-averaging this signal, and repeating for various values of
t_{\mathrm{ev}}
and each qubit state, produces the plotted data.

D.4
BC beam-splitter calibration (weak coupling regime)

As shown in Eq. (
94
), a beam-splitter interaction between modes B and C alters the effective dissipation rate of mode C. In addition, higher order nonlinearities of the dc SQUID result in a pump power-dependent frequency shift (rectification) of each mode from
\omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}}
and
\omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}}
to
\omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}}^{\prime}
and
\omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}}^{\prime}
respectively. The pump frequency and amplitude are thus interdependent, inspiring a calibration of both of these effects to reliably implement a BC beam-splitter interaction with a desired strength and detuning, as in the B mode thermal occupancy calibration of Sec.
IV.1
. To estimate the resonant beam-splitter pump frequency
\omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}}^{\mathrm{opt}}\vcentcolon=|\omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}}^{\prime}-\omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}}^{\prime}|
and strength
g_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}}
as a function of pump amplitude, we include a BC beam-splitter pump during the evolution time (
t_{\mathrm{ev}}
) of a catch-and-release measurement between modes B and C, as depicted in Fig.
13
(a). This alters the phase accumulation and increases the amplitude decay rate of the demodulated signal expressed in Eq. (
101
):

|z_{\mathrm{IQ}}(t_{\mathrm{ev}})|\propto e^{-\gamma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}},\mathrm{eff}}t_{\mathrm{ev}}/2},\qquad\arg(z_{\mathrm{IQ}}(t_{\mathrm{ev}}))=\Delta_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}},\mathrm{eff}}t_{\mathrm{ev}}.

(104)

First, we experimentally track
\Delta_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}},\mathrm{eff}}
. For this first measurement, we keep the frequency of the BC beam-splitter pump constant as we sweep its amplitude. This produces a value of
\Delta_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}},\mathrm{eff}}(s)
for each BC pump amplitude and qubit state (
s=-1,1
), shown in Fig.
13
(b). Using Eqs. (
92
) and (
94
), and generalizing mode C
\rightarrow
K, the qubit state-dependent pump frequency producing a resonant beam-splitter interaction between modes B and K (
\omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{K}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{K}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{K}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{K}$}}}^{\mathrm{opt}}(s)
) is given by

\omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{K}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{K}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{K}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{K}$}}}^{\mathrm{opt}}(s)\vcentcolon=|\omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{K}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{K}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{K}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{K}$}}}^{\prime}-\omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}}^{\prime}|\approx\omega_{p}+\mathrm{sgn}(\omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{K}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{K}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{K}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{K}$}}}-\omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}})\left[(\omega_{d}-\omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}}^{\prime})(1-4g_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{K}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{K}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{K}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{K}$}}}^{2}/\gamma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}}^{2})-\Delta_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{K}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{K}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{K}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{K}$}},\mathrm{eff}(s)}\right],

(105)

where the second term can be thought of as an effective pump detuning. This allows one to remove the unknown term
\omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}}^{\prime}
, however the other unknown terms of
\omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}}^{\prime}
and
g_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}}
remain. Here we will make a strong approximation and neglect the terms dependent on
\omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}}^{\prime}
and
g_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}}
. Indeed, by comparing the center of the feature in Fig.
16
with respect to drive frequency (a proxy for
\omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}}^{\prime}
), to the bare B mode frequency, we expect a maximal pump-induced B-mode rectification of approximately
\omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}}-\omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}}^{\prime}\lesssim 13
MHz, which is still substantially less than
\gamma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}}
. We therefore expect that the BC beam-splitter detunings resulting from this imperfect calibration do not substantially affect calibration of
g_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}}
discussed in the following paragraph, nor the dephasing dynamics in Fig.
5
(b). With the assumption of
\chi_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}}\approx 0
, a more accurate calibration would include an initial independent measurement of
\omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}}^{\prime}
using a detuned BC beam-splitter interaction (
\Delta_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}}\gg\gamma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}}
) to rectify the system with negligible beam-splitter dynamics. Subsequently, the B mode can be driven on-resonance for the catch-and-release measurements using the same BC beam-splitter pump strength employed to calibrate
\omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}}^{\prime}
(assuming negligible or calibrated-out flux-line ripple), effectively removing the term in Eq. (
105
) dependent on
g_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}}
.

After we perform the calibration of
\omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{K}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{K}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{K}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{K}$}}}^{\mathrm{opt}}(s)
, we employ these values in another catch-and-release protocol, altering the BC beam-splitter pump frequency as a function of its amplitude, to calibrate
g_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}}(s)
. Assuming the beam-splitter interaction is on-resonance for each amplitude and qubit state, we extract the beam-splitter coupling rate as

g_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}}(s)=\sqrt{\left(\gamma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}},\mathrm{eff}}(s)-\gamma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}}\right)\left(\gamma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}}/4+\chi_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}}^{2}/\gamma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}}\right)}.

(106)

The resulting values are plotted in Fig.
13
(c). It is once again worth noting that while the BC beam-splitter pump frequencies employed for the extraction of
g_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}}(s)
in Fig.
13
(c) were qubit-state dependent, the pump frequencies chosen for the B mode occupancy measurement were averaged with respect to the qubit state:
\omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}},\mathrm{avg}}^{\mathrm{opt}}=(1/2)(\omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}}^{\mathrm{opt}}(s=-1)+\omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}}^{\mathrm{opt}}(s=1))
.

Figure 13:
BC beam-splitter calibration
.
(a)
Diagram of the general experimental procedure, consisting of a tunable BC beam-splitter pump during the C mode evolution time (
t_{\mathrm{ev}}
) in a catch-and-release protocol.
(b)

\Delta_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}},\mathrm{eff}}
(defined in the main text), a quantity used to calibrate the pump-induced frequency shift, and
(c)

g_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}}
are both plotted as a function of the BC beam-splitter pump amplitude (in arbitrary units) and qubit state. We note that the measurement in (b) retains a static pump frequency, whereas the measurement in (c) varies the pump frequency as a function of amplitude to ideally keep the BC beam-splitter on-resonance. We fit the
g_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}}
data averaged over the qubit state with a quadratic function with respect to BC output amplitude for interpolation in future measurements.

D.5
Catch and release: AC beam-splitter calibration (strong coupling regime)

Similarly, to analyze the frequency shifts and parametric coupling rate of modes A and C in the presence of an AC beam-splitter pump, we include this pump during the evolution time
t_{\mathrm{ev}}
of the catch-and-release sequence, shown in Fig.
14
(a). To understand the signal output at the end of this measurement, we begin with the equations of motion for the mean fields
a\vcentcolon=\langle\hat{a}\rangle
,
c\vcentcolon=\langle\hat{c}\rangle
, including some beam-splitter detuning
\Delta_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{A}$}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}}\vcentcolon=\omega_{p}-\omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}}+\omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{A}$}}}
:

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}a

\displaystyle=\left(i\Delta_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{A}$}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}}-\frac{\gamma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{A}$}}}}{2}\right)a-ig_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{A}$}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}}c

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}c

\displaystyle=\left(is\chi_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}}-\frac{\gamma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}}}{2}\right)c-ig_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{A}$}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}}a

(107)

In the beginning of the catch-and-release protocol, we perform an effective drive on the C mode. Correspondingly assuming
a(t_{\mathrm{ev}}=0)=0
and
c(t_{\mathrm{ev}}=0)\neq 0
, the equations of motion for C can be solved as

c(t)\propto e^{-\gamma t_{\mathrm{ev}}/2}\left[\cos\left(\frac{\Omega t_{\mathrm{ev}}}{2}\right)-\frac{i(\Delta_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{A}$}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}}-s\chi_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}})}{\sqrt{\Omega}}\sin\left(\frac{\Omega t_{\mathrm{ev}}}{2}\right)\right],\qquad\Omega\vcentcolon=\sqrt{4g_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{A}$}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}}^{2}+(\Delta_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{A}$}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}}-s\chi_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}})^{2}}.

(108)

Following the logic of Appendix
D.2
, the magnitude of the released signal is

|z_{\mathrm{IQ}}(t_{\mathrm{ev}})|\propto e^{-\gamma t_{\mathrm{ev}}/2}\sqrt{\cos^{2}\left(\frac{\Omega t_{\mathrm{ev}}}{2}\right)+\frac{(\Delta_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{A}$}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}}-s\chi_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}})^{2}}{\Omega}\sin^{2}\left(\frac{\Omega t_{\mathrm{ev}}}{2}\right)}.

(109)

As a function of
t_{\mathrm{ev}}
, we therefore expect magnitude oscillations whose frequency grows as
g_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{A}$}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}}
increases, and whose amplitude grows and frequency diminishes as
\Delta_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{A}$}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}}\rightarrow s\chi_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}}
. Experimentally performing this catch-and-release sequence for various values of
t_{\mathrm{ev}}
and AC beam-splitter pump frequencies produces the colorplot in Fig.
14
(b) (for a fixed output pump amplitude of 0.25 [arb.] or
g_{\mathrm{AC}}\approx 1.7
MHz, in this case). The pump frequency and evolution time-dependence of the amplitude in Eq. (
109
) is captured in the chevron-like features of Fig.
14
(b). Taking a linecut at frequency
\omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{A}$}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}}^{\mathrm{opt}}
for which
\Delta_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{A}$}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}}=s\chi_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}}
, we recognize that

|z_{\mathrm{IQ}}(t_{\mathrm{ev}})|\propto e^{-\gamma t_{\mathrm{ev}}/2}|\cos(g_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{A}$}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}}t_{\mathrm{ev}})|.

(110)

Fitting this linecut data to Eq. (
110
) allows us to extract
g_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{A}$}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}}
for a given output AC pump amplitude. Repeating this procedure allows us to extract
\omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{A}$}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}}^{\mathrm{opt}}
and
g_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{A}$}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}}
as a function of AC pump amplitude, producing the data in Fig.
14
(c). To interpolate these points in subsequent measurements, we fit this data with a quadratic function for both parameters.

Figure 14:
AC beam-splitter calibration
.
(a)
Diagram of the general experimental procedure, consisting of an amplitude and frequency-varied AC beam-splitter pump during the C mode evolution time (
t_{\mathrm{ev}}
) in a catch-and-release protocol.
(b)
Magnitude of the signal released from the C mode over a 2D scan of AC beam-splitter frequency
\omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{A}$}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}}/2\pi
(y axis) and evolution time (x axis) at a beam-splitter amplitude of 0.25 [arb.] (
g_{\mathrm{AC}}\approx 1.7
MHz) for both qubit states.
(c)
Pump frequency required to achieve a resonant AC beam-splitter interaction,
\omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{A}$}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}}^{\mathrm{opt}}(s)
, and
(d)
AC beam-splitter interaction strength,
g_{\mathrm{AC}}
, plotted as a function of output pump strength in arbitrary amplitude units. We average each quantity with respect to the qubit state, and fit the average data to a quadratic function for interpolation in subsequent measurements.

D.6
Drive strength and
\chi_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}}
calibration

Figure 15:
(a)-(c) Drive strength
\varepsilon
and (d)-(f) spurious B dispersive shift
\chi_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}}
calibrations.

(a)
Diagram of the concurrent B mode drive and fixed BC beam-splitter (
g_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}}=7.075~\mathrm{MHz}
) sent during the qubit’s evolution in
T_{1}
and
T_{2}^{\mathrm{echo}}
measurements.
(b)
Measured qubit dephasing as a function of drive frequency
\omega_{d}
at fixed amplitude (5.0 [arb.]) to determine the drive frequency corresponding to a zero-detuning effective drive on the dressed C mode.
(c)
Measured qubit dephasing rate
\Gamma_{\mathrm{d}}
as a function of drive strength with a fixed drive frequency
\omega_{d}/2\pi=7.59
GHz, fit to find the coefficient between
\varepsilon
and arbitrary output amplitude.
(d)-(f)
is a repetition of (a)-(c), without the BC beam-splitter during the qubit’s evolution in
T_{1}
and
T_{2}^{\mathrm{echo}}
measurements. In the sweep of
\omega_{d}
shown in (e), we choose a drive output amplitude of 250.0 [arb.], and in the sweep over
\varepsilon
shown in (f) we set the drive frequency to
\omega_{d}/2\pi=7.585
GHz, and fit the resulting data for
\chi_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}}
. As indicated by the double-sided arrow, the fits of the data in (c) and (f) are interdependent and must be sequentially performed for continuously-updated values of
\varepsilon
and
\chi_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}}
.

To minimize the number of free parameters in the comparison between theory and experiment for the measurement
\Gamma_{\mathrm{meas}}
and dephasing
\Gamma_{\mathrm{d}}
rates in the main body, we separately calibrated the drive strength at the reference plane of the device (
\varepsilon
), as well as the spurious dispersive shift to mode B (
\chi_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}}
). We extract these parameters with iterative fits of codependent measurements using the full theory presented in Sec.
II
.

To calibrate
\varepsilon
, we monitor
\Gamma_{\mathrm{d}}
in the presence of a weak B mode drive (5.0 in arbitrary output amplitude units) and BC beam-splitter (
g_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}}=7.075
MHz), as shown in Fig.
15
(a). To ensure the effective C mode drive is approximately on-resonance, we operate in the regime
\gamma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}},\mathrm{eff}}\approx 2\chi_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}}
, and sweep the frequency of the weak B drive
\omega_{d}
to find where the qubit’s dephasing rate is maximal (assuming a resonant BC beam-splitter interaction). The data, shown in Fig.
15
(b), suggest an optimal drive frequency of approximately 7.59 GHz. We then hold the drive constant at this frequency, and monitor
\Gamma_{\mathrm{d}}
as we sweep the amplitude over a range of weak values (to prevent the appearance of self Kerr-induced dynamics). As shown in Fig.
15
(c),
\Gamma_{\mathrm{d}}
increases proportionally to the square of drive amplitude, per the expected behavior. Initially assuming
\chi_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}}=0
, we then numerically fit the data to the theory with one fit parameter, allowing us to extract a preliminary coefficient relating
\varepsilon
and output drive amplitude in arbitrary units.

However, in reality
\chi_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}}\neq 0
, a fact which contributes to further quadratic growth of the qubit’s dephasing rate as a function of drive amplitude. To disambiguate these two effects, we repeat the same pair of measurements without the BC beam-splitter. Due to the fact that
\gamma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}}\gg 2\chi_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}}
, much larger drive strengths are required to noticeably dephase the qubit. The sweep of
\omega_{d}
with a drive amplitude of 250.0 [arb.] is shown in Fig.
15
(e), resulting in a chosen drive frequency of
\omega_{d}/2\pi=7.585
GHz for the sweep of drive amplitude in Fig.
15
(f). The
\varepsilon
coefficient from the previous fit renormalizes the x axis, allowing for a single-free parameter fit for
\chi_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}}
.

Because of the interdependence of the fits for
\varepsilon
and
\chi_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}}
, we iteratively fit the data from each measurement over multiple rounds. We include the preliminary value of
\chi_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}}
as a static parameter in a second fit for the
\varepsilon
coefficient, producing a more accurate value which we then include as a static parameter in the fit for
\chi_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}}
. We repeat this process until the change in the
\varepsilon
coefficient fit parameter from one iteration to the next is within the confidence interval given by 1
\sigma
, which in our case takes three rounds of successive fitting. From this iterative procedure we extract
\varepsilon/(2\pi\times\mathrm{amp.})=183.0\pm 0.6
kHz/arb., and
\chi_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}}=236
kHz.

D.7
Calibrating noise of the subsequent measurement chain,
\bar{n}_{\mathrm{rest}}

To compare the experimentally-measured and theoretically-computed
\Gamma_{\mathrm{meas}}
for the case of the interferometer in Sec.
IV.2
with no additional fit parameters, we must independently characterize the added noise of the measurement chain downstream from the readout network,
\bar{n}_{\mathrm{add}}
. We extract this value by probing
\Gamma_{\mathrm{meas}}
(see Fig.
4
(b)) in the presence of a BC beam-splitter of strength
g_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}}=7.075
MHz and a B mode drive of strength
\varepsilon=2.77
MHz. For this configuration,
\Gamma_{\mathrm{meas}}=32.732
kHz. Utilizing the theory from Sec.
II
with this value and the previously-calibrated linewidths, thermal occupancies, and cross Kerrs tabulated in Fig.
3
(c) and Table
1
, we performed a numerical inversion of
\Gamma_{\mathrm{m}}(\bar{n}_{\mathrm{add}})
to extract
\bar{n}_{\mathrm{rest}}=5.24
photons.

D.8
Mismatched circulator tuning

The strategies discussed in Appendices
D.4
and
D.5
can be used to calibrate the coupling strength and frequency rectification from a single beam-splitter pump. However, when adding multiple pumps of various frequencies, the rectification effects combine in a manner dependent on all of the higher-order nonlinearities of each mode – a calibration of which is outside the scope of this work. Because of this, we use a separate heuristic to account for the rectification-induced shifts in beam-splitter pump frequencies and strengths when operating the interferometer in Sec.
IV.2
.

As mentioned in Appendix
D.1
, the Fano interference present in our system renders the steady-state reflection coefficient off of mode B (
S_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}}
) difficult to fit using coupled-mode theory. Luckily, the fields that bypass the reflection off of the device and interfere with our measurement signal are independent of the qubit state. With this in mind, a reasonable heuristic for diagnosing multiple pump-induced shifts is an effective qubit measurement signal
S_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}},\mathrm{diff}}\equiv S_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}},\ket{e}}-S_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}},\ket{g}}
. By sweeping both the interferometer phase
\phi
, as well as the drive frequency sent to the B mode
\omega_{d}
, the magnitude of the qubit signal
\left|S_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}},\mathrm{diff}}\right|
provides visual features whose characteristics vary as a function of pump frequency and strength.

To theoretically analyze the qubit measurement signal, we compute
S_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}},\ket{\sigma}}
, where
\sigma=e,g
, for the interferometer with the linear-response theory presented in Appendix
A.4
, which is equivalent to coupled mode theory in this system
Ranzani and Aumentado 2015
;
Lecocq et al. 2017
;
Lecocq et al. 2020
. We then took the magnitude of the difference between the two qubit state-dependent reflection coefficients
|S_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}},\mathrm{diff,theory}}|\vcentcolon=A\left|S_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}},\ket{e},\mathrm{theory}}-S_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}},\ket{g},\mathrm{theory}}\right|
where
A
is an arbitrary prefactor to account for the total gain level of our measurement path. We then tune the following quantities in the theory until there is a substantial match with the data:

•

amplitude prefactor on qubit signal,

•

experimental offset in interferometric phase (up to
\pi
),

•

assumed rectified frequencies of A, B, and C modes (this parameterizes AB, BC, and AC pump frequencies),

•

parametric coupling strengths
g_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{A}$}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}}
,
g_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}}
, and
g_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{A}$}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}}
.

All in all, there are 8 free parameters in this fit. In theory, it is possible to constrain
A
from our independent measurements of
\varepsilon
and
\bar{n}_{\mathrm{rest}}
in Appendices
D.6
and
D.7
respectively. However, we found that this parameter, along with the experimental offset in interferometer phase, were orthogonal from the rest of the parameters and didn’t contribute to the tuning complexity. In the tuning process, we found more success performing this fit “by hand” compared to defining the cost function as
\left||S_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}},\mathrm{diff}}|-|S_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}},\mathrm{diff,theory}}|\right|
and performing a simple numerical minimization. This seems to be due to the large number of local minima in the cost function with respect to the free parameters; however, more sophisticated methods of fitting are worth exploring.

Once we fit the data, we use the results to steer the experimental pump parameters toward those desired for ideal device operation. Because of the aforementioned rectification effects, the ideal pump amplitudes and frequencies are interdependent, necessitating small changes in pump amplitude and frequency for each fit iteration. The final data/theory comparison resulting from this fitting and steering procedure is shown in Fig.
16
. The theory contains zero pump detunings, and strengths of
(g_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{A}$}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}},g_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}},g_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{A}$}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}})/2\pi=(7.98,14.5,6.2)
MHz.

Figure 16:
Mismatched circulator qubit signal
. Measured data and theory of the qubit signal,
\left|S_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}},\ket{e}}-S_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}},\ket{g}}\right|
plotted against the probe tone frequency
\omega_{d}/2\pi
(y axis) and interferometer phase
\phi
(x axis). An arbitrary coefficient scales qubit signal theory to account for the net gain of the signal path. We employ this heuristic of qubit measurement signal to find beam-splitter detunings and deviations from ideal coupling rates caused by rectification.

Appendix E
Diagnostic qubit readout

Regardless of the readout network’s operation, a standard configuration to read out the qubit’s state is necessary to perform the
T_{1}
and
T_{2}^{\mathrm{echo}}
measurements. In principle, this readout could be performed with a reflection measurement off of mode C. However, the Purcell filtering scheme we have chosen (see Appendix
H
) limits us to high SNR measurements only in reflection off of mode B. However, a dispersive measurement of the qubit using mode B directly would also be low fidelity as
\gamma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}}\gg\chi_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}}
.

We therefore include a single BC beam-splitter interaction to effectively increase the external coupling rate of the C mode. This can be seen by taking Eq. (
93
) for the case of K
\rightarrow
C:

\frac{d}{dt}c=\left(is\chi_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}},\mathrm{eff}}-\frac{\gamma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}},\mathrm{eff}}}{2}\right)c+\varepsilon

(111)

where

\gamma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}},\mathrm{eff}}:=\frac{g^{2}}{\gamma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}}^{2}/4+\chi_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}}^{2}}\gamma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}}+\gamma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}},\qquad\chi_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}},\mathrm{eff}}:=\chi_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}}-\frac{g^{2}}{\gamma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}}^{2}/4+\chi_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}}^{2}}\chi_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}}.

(112)

Assuming
\chi_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}}\ll\chi_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}},\gamma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}}
and negligible parasitic dephasing, the total qubit dephasing rate
\Gamma_{\mathrm{d}}\approx\Gamma_{\mathrm{d,m}}
can be estimated from Eq. (
71
):

\Gamma_{\mathrm{d,m}}\approx\frac{4n_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}},\mathrm{eff}}\chi_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}}^{2}\gamma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}}(4g^{2}+\gamma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}}\gamma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}})}{(4g^{2}+\gamma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}}\gamma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}})^{2}+4\gamma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}}^{2}\chi_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}}^{2}}

(113)

where the effective intracavity photon number is given by

n_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}},\mathrm{eff}}=\frac{4|\varepsilon|^{2}}{(4g^{2}/\gamma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}}+\gamma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}})^{2}+\chi_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}}^{2}}.

(114)

Assuming that we hold
n_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}},\mathrm{eff}}
constant by sweeping
|\varepsilon|
alongside
g
, maximizing Eq. (
113
) with respect to
g
produces

g_{\mathrm{max}(\Gamma_{\mathrm{d,m}})}=\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{\gamma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}}(2\chi_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}}-\gamma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}})}\quad\Rightarrow\quad\gamma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}},\mathrm{eff}}=2\chi_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}}.

(115)

Because
\Gamma_{\mathrm{meas}}\propto\Gamma_{\mathrm{d,m}}
for this case, maximal fidelity occurs at maximum
\Gamma_{\mathrm{d,m}}
. In our device, this condition is met for
g=7.25
MHz. For diagnostic qubit measurement, we therefore set the beam-splitter coupling to this value, aided by the BC strength calibration discussed in Appendix
D.4
.

In the experimental sequence for diagnostic qubit readout, we pulse this beam-splitter interaction with a duration of 550 ns, beginning concurrently with a B mode drive which lasts 500 ns. Each pulse has 50 ns
\cos^{2}
rise/fall envelopes. We digitally downconvert the resulting signal to an IF frequency of 90 MHz and subsequently digitize it. To obtain a time-weighted averaging function that maximizes qubit readout SNR, we repeat this measurement
10^{4}
times for each state of the qubit with a passive-reset delay between each shot. We then take the difference of the resulting shot-averaged time traces with respect to the qubit’s state, producing the weighted averaging function (including oscillations at the IF frequency) shown in Fig.
17
(b). To quantify the readout and active reset fidelity
\mathcal{F}_{r}
, we prepare and measure the qubit’s ground or excited state, then actively reset the qubit to ground at the end of the sequence. We repeat this procedure every 5.36
\mu s
(which is a fraction of
T_{1}\approx 26\mu s
) over
10^{4}
shots for each qubit state, prepared in an interleaved manner. We then threshold the resulting single shot values to extract a single-shot readout and reset fidelity
\mathcal{F}_{r}=1-\mathrm{P}(g|e)-\mathrm{P}(e|g)=0.952
. This fidelity includes assignment, state preparation, and QND (i.e. relaxation and measurement-drive excitation) errors within the two-state manifold. A simplified diagram of the measurement and active reset sequence, and the single shot histograms are shown in Fig.
17
(a) and (c) respectively.

Appendix F
Spurious Gain

While attempting to introduce a single-mode squeezing interaction in mode A, we noticed a spurious gain process that overwhelmed the desired interaction. To demonstrate this, in Fig.
18
, we plot the measured and simulated qubit measurement signal
S_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}},\mathrm{diff}}
as a function of B mode drive frequency and interferometer phase (as in Appendix
D.8
), for two different drive powers (
\varepsilon=0.4625,1.85
MHz). This beam-splitter pump configuration is the same as the interferometer
(g_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{A}$}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}},g_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}},g_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{A}$}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{C}$}}})/2\pi=(7.98,14.5,6.2)
MHz, with an additional pump at
2\omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{A}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{A}$}}}
. In the theory, we set the squeezing strength to
\lambda/2\pi=2
MHz (corresponding to
\sim 0.85
dB of gain at
\phi=\pm\pi/2
) to qualitatively match the data and remain approximately consistent with the measured gain from the idler of mode A’s single mode squeezing process (see next paragraph). Despite this, the
\sim 5
dB gain features around
\phi\approx\pm\pi/2
and
\omega_{d}\approx\omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}}^{\prime}
were not reproducible in the theory, even over a large range of beam-splitter detunings and strengths. For measurements with larger gain-pump amplitudes, this spurious process dominates the dynamics of the device, quickly bringing it into an unstable regime.

Figure 18:
Spurious gain in embedded amplifier
. Measured qubit signal (
\left|S_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}},\ket{e}}-S_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}},\ket{g}}\right|
) data with a drive amplitude of
(a)

\varepsilon=0.4625
MHz and
(b)

\varepsilon=1.85
MHz, and
(c)
theory plotted as a function of the probe tone frequency
\omega_{d}/2\pi
(y-axis) and interferometric phase
\phi
(x-axis).

Comparing Fig.
18
(a) and (b), these spurious gain features do not change when we change the drive amplitude by a factor of four (
\approx 12
dB). It is therefore unlikely to be a signature of self-Kerr of the modes, or cross-Kerr between any of the modes, due to the presence of the SQUID. In an attempt to identify the idler of this gain process, we analyzed the output spectrum of the device and identified numerous peaks. If we included a slightly off-resonance B mode drive, we could identify a small peak corresponding to the idler of the weak single-mode squeezing at mode A. From the difference in signal and idler peak heights, we estimated the gain from this process to be about 1 dB before the onset of instability from the other spurious process. We identified all other present peaks as spurious tones generated by the room temperature setup, which did not appreciably affect this spurious process when suppressed via filtering or active cancellation. Note that our spectrum analysis was limited to the bandwidth of the Purcell filter: 7.25 - 7.75 GHz, and it is very possible that the idler of the spurious process is present outside that bandwidth.

Appendix G
Device design and simulation details

In this section we include details regarding the finite-element simulation of the device described in Sec.
III.2
.

G.1
Eigenmode simulation results

To simulate the frequencies, linewidths, and the qubit junction’s participation in all of the modes in the device, we used the COMSOL Eigenfrequency module. To compute the qubit’s anharmonicity and cross-Kerr with each mode, we followed the energy participation ratio method detailed in Ref.
Minev et al. 2021
. These result in the electric field profiles plotted in Fig.
19
, and the simulated device parameters summarized in Table
2
for the operation flux of
\Phi=0.246\Phi_{0}
.

mode

\omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{K}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{K}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{K}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{K}$}}}/2\pi

\gamma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{K}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{K}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{K}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{K}$}}}/2\pi

\chi_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{K}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{K}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{K}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{K}$}}}/2\pi

Table 2:
EM simulation outputs at
\Phi=0.246\Phi_{0}
. Note, modes A, C, and Q have port boundary conditions in the simulation that differs from the experiment due to external filtering of the I/O and flux lines at the respective frequencies.

It is worth noting that experimentally, the frequencies of modes A, C, and Q lie outside the bandwidth of the circulators and filtered drive and flux lines. These modes will therefore see a different boundary condition than the 50 Ohm environment enforced at both ports in the simulation, leading to potentially noticeable discrepancies in the simulated and measured parameters for these modes. In general, the frequencies of the simulated modes slightly underestimate the measured device frequencies. The simulation overestimates the linewidth of mode B, and estimates the measured A and C mode linewidths relatively well both with and without the Purcell filter (see Fig.
3
(c) and Fig.
11
respectively). The simulation underestimates the qubit’s self Kerr (anharmonicity) and cross Kerr with mode B, but overestimates the cross Kerr with mode C. Some of these discrepancies could be due the small detuning between the qubit and the C mode in the COMSOL model, as the qubit frequency without the loading of the junction nonlinearity is 8.566 GHz. The simulation may therefore overestimate the degree of hybridization of the qubit and C mode, diluting the nonlinearity of the qubit, and increasing its dispersive coupling with mode C.

Figure 19:
Simulated electric field mode profiles
for the cavity (C), buffer (B), and amplifier (A) modes.

G.2
I/O and qubit drive port

The model for the charge-driven I/O and qubit-drive port is shown in Fig.
20
. We model this port as a 50
\Omega
coaxial line with a center conductor radius 0.65 mm, PTFE (
\epsilon_{r}=2.1
) dielectric sleeve with an outer radius of 2.05 mm, a length of 2 cm, and a TEM boundary condition, which may be excited to simulate the device’s driven response. The center pin is connected to an on-chip pad with three modeled wirebonds of radius 25
\mu
m. This pad connects to a meander inductor, which provides the inductance for a single-pole, series LC filter with a simulated center frequency of 5.98 GHz and linewidth of 1.47 GHz, setting some degree of Purcell protection for the qubit and the A and C modes. The capacitance of this single-pole filter is provided by two capacitors in parallel below the meander inductor, which were also designed to destructively interfere the differential voltages on the interdigitated capacitor (IDC) leads for the A mode excitation. This minimizes mode A’s coupling to the I/O port, without substantially sacrificing the external coupling rate of mode B, due to the fact that the two modes excite different field profiles within the IDC.

Figure 20:
I/O and qubit drive port
. COMSOL model of charge-coupled port to act as the I/O port and qubit drive. Also pictured are the meander inductor, and two parallel capacitors connected to the terminals of a separate IDC, as described in the text.

G.3
Flux biasing

We model the flux port as a finite coplanar waveguide (CPW) with a 50
\Omega
lumped resistor shorting the center trace to ground. This CPW transitions to a differential excitation in a coplanar stripline (CPS) via a balun, as shown in Fig.
21
(a). The CPS then travels up the chip where it is inductively shorted in a loop with mutual inductance to the dc SQUID, as shown in Fig.
21
(b). This coupled line can therefore induce DC and AC circulating currents in the SQUID, facilitating parametric processes.

Figure 21:
Flux line
. COMSOL model of on-chip flux line to bias the dc SQUID and enable parametric interactions consisting of
(a)
a finite coplanar waveguide (CPW) with a lumped 50
\Omega
resistor shorting the center conductor to ground, transitioning to a coplanar stripline (CPS) via a balun, and
(b)
an inductive short in the form of a loop with mutual inductance to the dc SQUID.

To model the effective SQUID inductance, we apply the following expression for an asymmetric dc SQUID:

L_{\mathrm{SQUID}}(\Phi)=\frac{L_{0}}{\sqrt{\alpha^{2}+(1-\alpha^{2})\cos^{2}(\pi\Phi/\Phi_{0})}}

(116)

where
\Phi
is the external magnetic flux threading the loop,
\Phi_{0}
is the magnetic flux quantum,
L_{0}
is the unbiased SQUID inductance, and
\alpha
is an asymmetry parameter of the junctions’ critical currents,
I_{c,1}=I_{c}(1-\alpha)
and
I_{c,2}=(1+\alpha)
. In the finite-element simulation, the dc SQUID is modeled as a loop with two lumped inductors representing the junctions. As the lumped inductors are in parallel, each is assigned the inductance
2\times L_{\mathrm{SQUID}}
in the simulation.

G.4
Other simulation parameters

In the model for the device, the values of unbiased dc SQUID inductance
L_{0}
and transmon junction inductance
L_{J}
are extracted from room-temperature resistance measurements. We tuned the dielectric constant of sapphire in the finite-element model in an effort to match the measured qubit anharmonicity (see next subsection) until it reached the lower bound of the acceptable range for sapphire (
\epsilon_{r}
= 9.3). Subject to variations from machining and fabrication tolerances, the simulated geometry and external flux bias otherwise match the implemented device parameters. The relevant simulated device parameter inputs are summarized in Table
3
.

Appendix H
Experimental diagram

Below is a diagram of the experimental setup utilized in this work. We send attenuated drives at the buffer and qubit frequencies to the charge-coupled port of the readout network. For the three beam-splitter interactions (AB, BC, and AC), we send attenuated signals to the flux line of the device, as well as tee in a DC bias at the mixing chamber, allowing the pumps to turn on three-wave mixing processes. Signals traveling from the device pass through a low-noise microwave measurement chain enabled by a TWPA. Notably, we place external filters to protect leakage at frequencies outside the buffer mode for the signal line, and at frequencies above the pump band for the flux line. Drives outside the signal line’s bandpass Purcell filter are still permitted to reach the device through the coupled port of a directional coupler placed between the filter and the device. The finite directivity of this coupler (
\sim
25 dB), which could be further degraded due to possible impedance mismatches, likely creates the Fano interference features that make an accurate fit of the scattering parameters difficult to achieve (necessitating the catch-and-release calibrations detailed in Appendix
D.2
). For the preliminary scattering parameter fits which produce the data shown in Figs.
3
(c) and
11
, we replace the Purcell bandpass filter and directional coupler within the device shield with a single-stage circulator outside the device shield.

Figure 22:
Cryogenic wiring diagram
utilized to deliver to the device drives at the buffer and qubit frequencies, as well as the DC flux bias and three parametric pumps at the AB, BC, and AC beam-splitter frequencies. Outgoing signals near
\omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{B}$}}}
are captured with low added noise through the use of a TWPA.
